- K943: no withdrawal act for a ratified case edition for now; the retraction question is the design stream's (see the DEFER that follows).

## B40 · NOTICE · 2026-10-05 · session_01MWqyw89yDMqxpG2iqqSom6 · primary
K1502 (Bob's, replaces K1478 (ii)): Anthropic's terms let a Claude subscription serve only its holder, so there is no group-wide or project-wide Claude account. Each member who wants the assistant connects their own Claude subscription (Anthropic's own sign-in) or their own API key, serving only their own asks, runs and standing questions; a member with neither has no assistant. The D311 disclosure (B26) moves from the administrator at setup to each member when they connect their account: their questions and the material read go to Anthropic under their own account. The suggestion switch (K1479) and a standing question's switch are the member's own; the use ceiling is per member.

## B49 · QUESTION · 2026-10-06 · session_01Kh2Gce1YCzgFDoLQvpt6pC · primary
Bob's ruling K1618 (2026-10-06, through BOB): a document captured for one member's question and set aside by another must not vanish silently. Bob approved this direction and asks the design stream for the screen decisions (a DEC):

1. **Context for the reviewer.** The held-captures list (capture R77) and the set-aside act (R79) show, for each held document, the question it was captured for and the member who asked it (the request's `target` and `principal_plane` are already recorded; capture-requests R8, R10).
2. **A reason when it matters.** Setting aside a document that an open question waits on asks for a reason, kept on the record.
3. **The asker sees it on the question, not as a notification** (DEC-94 stands): the question shows a wait in DEC-98's form, e.g. "Waiting: the 2024 budget PDF was set aside by Ben: 'wrong year'".
4. **Disagreement stays visible.** The asker can restore it with a reason (R81) or seek another source; each act is on the record. No veto or quorum rule for documents.

Gap found in the canon (none of this is defined today): no inquiry state for "waiting on a set-aside document", no link from a set-aside to a question, the held list shows no requester. BOB folds the requirement changes (capture, capture-requests, inquiry, intent pursuit view) into the next tranche once your DEC is on `main`; please answer with the DEC number, or DEFER any part back.

You need to think about the mindset of the audiences are. When I name the group, there's no visual acknowledgement that the name has been accepted. The first sentence of 'Your group's website' is "Only if your group has a website you can put a file on." If you think about the mindset of a newly minted group administrator, that sentence makes no sense. Again, the text explaining 'Your place, if it is not listed' makes not sense to the audience. Same for 'Offices and seats', 'A note about it', and many others. Really, the whole narrative needs to be reconsidered and rewritten. If an admin indicates to keep AI out, then everything else related to AI should collapse and disappear from the setup. The layout and presentation of the entire setup page needs to be cleaned up significantly to match the UX of all other Civicsmith surfaces. Sections (like AI, cyber-security, and others) should be visually delineated, and be collapsable/expandable based on choices being made. Mouseovers and other means of appropriately increasing information richness. The goal is best practices, minimum visible complexity, welcoming, powerful, exciting."

This is UX, and so yours with Bob (DEC). Asked of the design stream: the setup screen's narrative rewritten for a newly made administrator (every `screen.setup.*` sentence, `screen.setup.domain.help` among them), and its drawing redone in the kit of the other screens: delineated, collapsible sections opened by the choices made (keeping AI out collapses every AI part), an acknowledgement when the group's name is accepted, and hover help. The screen is screens-entry's (`bio-plane/src/screens-entry/setup.mjs`); BOB folds your DEC into its requirements as N1152 for T50's screens-entry job. Please post a NOTICE with the DEC when it is drawn.

## B332 · NOTICE · 2026-10-18 · session_01GSmA9F4nLx4fViJzQfU6f7 · primary
K3930 (Bob, 2026-10-18): how words are kept is B, extraction, on four conditions: "B with the four conditions. Be smart about implementing this change. Do it correctly given the long-term objectives for Civicsmith development." (1) Fixed ids, assigned once, never changed; every existing words.json key is kept as an id. (2) The table always wins: a sentence written first in code as t(id, en) answers your row once words.json holds that id, and code wording is never synced back, so a wording change of yours never touches a module. (3) You write first on drawn screens (screens-*, interface-shell, wizard-scripts, site, the installer's pages read only word(key)); every other new sentence is written first in code and reviewed by you before each release: the generated list docs/development/ux-substrate/screens/words-unreviewed.json (id, en, module, file, line) is your review queue, sent as a HANDOFF at each tranche close; you review a row by writing it into words.json under the same id, as is or reworded, with a translator's note. The release's words check fails while a row is unreviewed. (4) The conversion is proven by an unchanged full test run, a character-for-character comparison and a pseudo-locale walk. Message syntax is ICU MessageFormat ({name} today; plural and select refused until rendered). BOB's design: build/plan/words-B-design.md on tranche/T51 (K3931). What changes for you: the missing-row batch of K3919 shrinks to the drawn screens' keys only; the ~4,500 unkeyed sentences no longer need rows before code reads them. Nothing to answer unless you see a problem with condition 3's split; an ACK is enough.
